- **iter 117 = reliability observability (operator fix #2 of 3) -- NEW read-only `foundry stage-times` per-stage attempt-duration CLI over `dispatcher.out`.** OPERATOR-SET 2026-08-04 names the ~600s per-stage agent-CLI cap the #1 cause of lost shifts and says "THE FOUNDRY IS BLIND TO IT"; fix #1 (WRITE-EARLY in role cards) shipped iter 112 (`e81b95e`), so #2 (make the budget observable) is the highest operator-priority remaining reliability work. **What ships (ADDITIVE-DORMANT, same class as `timing`/`directions`/`novelty-check`):** a new module constant `STAGE_SOFT_BUDGET` (default 420s, patchable, read at call time); a pure total `parse_stage_attempts(text)` that pairs each `**STAGE** attempt A started` line with its next `STAGE produced`/`STAGE no output file` terminal for the same (team,iter,stage) into non-negative int `duration_s` + a `produced` bool (never raises); a pure `summarize_stage_times(attempts, budget=None)` grouping by (team,stage) into count/median/max/timeouts(no-output attempts)/over_budget (`median > STAGE_SOFT_BUDGET`); a frozen summary with `.render()`/`.to_dict()`/`.exit_code` (2 no-data / 1 >=1 over budget / 0 clean); a `gather_stage_times(log, budget=None, team=None)` seam (missing/unreadable log -> empty summary); and a read-only `foundry stage-times [--log PATH] [--team NAME] [--budget N] [--json]` CLI dispatched BEFORE `load_config` (no product `--config`; `dispatcher.out` is a foundry-root artifact, default `FOUNDRY / "dispatcher.out"`). DORMANT: no call site in `run_iteration`/`run_stage`/dispatch loop, writes nothing, resume-safe. **Triage:** chosen (Scout B B1) OVER Scout A C1 (`foundry init` scaffold renderer, biggest raw vision gap): section-8 discovery gate LIFTED (bites 1-4 all shipped, iter 116), so both are un-gated; B1 delivers an operator-actionable stall PREDICTION for the #1 lost-shift cause THIS iteration at strictly lower risk, whereas C1's bite-1 is a preview-only renderer whose value lands only in a future write-class bite -- operator-ranked priority beats a scout's raw-gap value read. Also OVER Scout B B2 (doctor stale-IPC advisory: that failure mode's auto-heal already landed iter 114, so B2 only warns where the operator looks). Strangler step 5 (delete dead inline copies) flagged NEAR-EMPTY by both scouts -> deferred. NOVELTY verdict = VARIED (no rut). **Distinct from `timing`** (which reports the fresh-clone SUITE wall-time from `postrelease.md`, not per-STAGE attempt durations). **Out of scope:** wiring the WARN into `doctor`/a role card (a separate later bite; the read-only-first pattern matches `novelty-check`); operator fix #3 (stream child stdout on a 600s kill -- touches the live control path); no `company-stage-times` roll-up. Ship set = {foundry.py, README.md (index #42), PLATFORM_ROADMAP.md, new tests/test_iter117_behavior.py}. 10 behaviors, offline, lint-spec OK.

- **iter 118 = HARDENING of item 2, the SECOND and last half of the digest char budget -- close the `## Patterns` HEAD EXEMPTION (operator-labelled "highest-value platform pick", 2026-08-04).** iter 104 gave the inlined `learnings_digest` a real CHARACTER budget for the LESSON TAIL and deliberately left the head verbatim ("The `## Patterns` head stays verbatim"); foundry.py:896 documents the hole: "the head is NEVER truncated or budgeted". MEASURED live this iteration: prompt digest 21,846 chars = head **13,810** + bounded tail **7,998**, so the tail is COMPLIANT (under its 10,000 budget) and **100% of the 2.2x breach is the exempted head** -- 17 bullets whose six largest (3,191 / 2,323 / 1,513 / 1,437 / 1,210 / 1,004) are all operator-appended directives. The exemption was granted because the head was "curated and small"; it is now 63% of the digest and is the ONLY region with an unbounded writer -- the foundry's own instance of "whatever a gate exempts is where the gate can never look", in the hot path (the digest is ~92% of every stage prompt, re-sent ~7x per iteration, and prompt size drives time-to-first-token = upstream of the 600s stage timeouts the operator names the #1 cause of lost shifts). **What ships (purely additive, mirrors iter 104's own opt-in shape):** two new patchable constants `PROMPT_LEARNINGS_HEAD_BULLET_CHARS`=800 (symmetric with the lesson cap) and `PROMPT_LEARNINGS_HEAD_BUDGET_CHARS`=10000 (symmetric with the lesson budget); two new OPTIONAL `learnings_digest` params `head_bullet_chars` / `head_chars`, both defaulting to `None` = today's byte-identical verbatim head, so `learnings_cli` / `learnings_view` / `render_agents_md` and ~40 existing digest tests are untouched; per-BULLET-BLOCK truncation reusing `_truncate_lesson` (multi-line bullets truncate as ONE block, exact-cap + `" [...]"` marker) applied FIRST, then a top-down whole-block admission within the total head budget (drops from the BOTTOM so the self-declared highest-precedence first bullet always survives), plus ONE loud greppable notice line emitted IFF something was elided; `build_prompt` (the sole cost driver) opts in, reading both constants as module globals at call time. Adds the budget ORACLE iter 104 only half-delivered (`len(digest) <= head_chars + max_chars + 300` on pathological input) and a live-log regression expressed as INEQUALITIES (no hard-coded char count -- the log grows every iteration). **SIMULATED against the live log before speccing:** 6 of 17 bullets truncate, 0 drop, head 13,811 -> 7,934, digest -> 15,968 = **5,878 chars (26.9%) off every stage prompt**; the drop path does NOT fire today, which is the intended division of labor -- the cap saves, the budget only guards. **Triage:** chosen (Scout A C2) OVER Scout B B1 (tracked `DIRECTIONS.md` stale at HEAD -- hardest evidence + smallest diff on either slate, but a narrative artifact, the THIRD consecutive visit to the `directions` area, and its own scout concedes slice (b) may change nothing since gate 4's prose rule was already ignored). Also OVER Scout A C1 (cause-aware retry backoff: biggest headline number at ~7.9h of 12.2h backoff sleep, but bite 1 is DORMANT = zero value this iteration, bite 2 edits `run_stage` retry semantics, and under congestion a 600s timeout IS congestion-caused -- C2 attacks the CAUSE where C1 attacks the COST, so prompt-shrink comes first) and Scout A C3 (fast test cmd: `_platform`'s own suite is ~32s, value speculative until a large product onboards). NOVELTY = VARIED. Resume-safe: prompts are rebuilt from source every stage and never persisted (iter 104's own argument); no control-flow / dispatcher / sentinel / CLI change; takes effect at the running dispatcher's next restart. **Out of scope:** no new verb or flag so NO new README `# N.` entry; no change to the existing RECENT/BUDGET/LESSON constants; no curation of the head CONTENT (operator-owned); not automating the drop path. Ship set = {foundry.py, ARCHITECTURE.md, PLATFORM_ROADMAP.md, new tests/test_iter118_behavior.py}. 13 behaviors, offline, lint-spec OK. **Next candidates:** Scout A C1 cause-aware backoff (bite 1 dormant), then A/C3 per-product fast test command, then B1 DIRECTIONS.md freshness.

- **iter 119 = reliability: ATTEMPT-AWARE RETRY -- the retry loop stops re-sending a byte-identical prompt (attacks the operator's #1 lost-shift cause at its cause, not its cost).** `run_stage` builds the prompt ONCE (foundry.py:11572) and re-substitutes that identical string into `agent_cmd` on every attempt of `for attempt in range(1, MAX_ATTEMPTS + 1)`, paying `BACKOFFS = [600, 1200, 2400]` between them, so nothing tells attempt 4 that attempts 1-3 died. MEASURED this iteration from the live `dispatcher.out`: 48 zero-output attempts across the 3 live teams, of which **33 ran out of time (>=540s, the agent CLI's own ~600s hard kill)**, 13 failed mid-flight and only 2 were instant fails; 17 of 25 affected stage-runs were eventually rescued by a later attempt but **8 produced nothing at all** (4 x ~600s of agent time + 4,200s of backoff each = ~14.7h). The loop's weakness is therefore NOT a missing retry but an unchanged prompt on every attempt. **What ships:** a pure, total, module-level `retry_directive(attempt, stage, out_file)` returning `""` for attempt < 2 and, for attempts 2+, a CHARACTER-BOUNDED block (`RETRY_DIRECTIVE_MAX_CHARS` <= 2000, ellipsis-truncated, marker constant `RETRY_DIRECTIVE_MARKER`) that states `attempt N of MAX_ATTEMPTS` (MAX_ATTEMPTS read at CALL TIME), names the stage + the exact required output file, reports that the previous attempt produced NO output file inside the cap, and steers the retry to write the file first as minimal-but-complete, cut scope, and inspect what earlier attempts already left in the tree. **Call site = exactly ONE line** inside the existing `{prompt}` comprehension in `run_stage`, by BARE name so a monkeypatch bites: `(prompt + retry_directive(attempt, stage, out_file)) if a == "{prompt}" else a`. Attempt 1 stays BYTE-IDENTICAL to today (the required test pins it), so the change is invisible on every happy path and can only fire where the loss already happens. **Triage:** chosen (Scout B B1) OVER Scout A C1 (define all six scout lenses in `roles/pm_scout.md` + retire the dead two-lens mapping still asserted in `roles/pm.md` and ARCHITECTURE.md -- real correctness defect, ships an oracle, but its own risk section concedes no number moves) and OVER Scout B B2 (per-product fast build-stage test command: its scout FALSIFIED the motivating evidence -- repolens has no STOP file, is `enabled: true`, and shipped twice on 08-05 with fresh-clone suites of 99.72s / 102.00s after a product-side `-n auto` fix in `e54364b`, so the pinned learnings-head claim that it is retired with a 498s suite is STALE), B3 (`foundry init` scaffold: real VISION gap, but the first write-capable CLI = a bigger surface), A/C2 (fourth consecutive visit to the `directions` area) and A/C3 (README split; already lost in iter 118). NOVELTY = VARIED. **Distinct from iter 118's rejected cause-aware backoff:** this changes the retry PROMPT, not the retry TIMING -- `MAX_ATTEMPTS`, `BACKOFFS`, `STAGE_TIMEOUT` and the non-empty-output-file success criterion are all untouched, which is what keeps it inside the small-reversible bar. **Out of scope:** reading/summarising the previous attempt's LOG into the prompt (the dominant class leaves a 48-byte timeout line, so there is nothing to mine); streaming child stdout; stage-resume (iteration numbers derive from the state dir, so it would change resume semantics); no new verb, flag, config field, README entry or runtime artifact. Ship set = {foundry.py, PLATFORM_ROADMAP.md, new tests/test_iter119_behavior.py}. 11 behaviors, offline, lint-spec OK. **Next candidates:** A/C1 six-lens scout-card fix (NOTE for its spec: `tests/test_iter81_behavior.py` asserts the literals `2-3`, `new-capability` and `hardening/DX` are present in `roles/pm_scout.md`, so widening that card is a spec collision to declare up front), then A/C2 candidate-heading predicate, then B3 `foundry init`.

- **iter 64 = roadmap item 18, bite 1 -- the NEW read-only `foundry lint-manifest --file <path> [--bench-dir <dir>] [--json]` staffing-manifest validator.** RECOVERED in iteration 124: this bullet was owed by the iteration that followed 64 and was never written, so from `eee06c1` until iter 124 the ONLY surviving record of this ship anywhere in the repo was its commit subject -- the chronic loss that motivated the third roadmap brake (see iter 124 below). Reconstructed from the commit itself (`eee06c1`, +944 lines: `foundry.py` +363, `tests/test_iter64_behavior.py` +579, `README.md` +2) and from that shipped test module's own contract, NOT from memory. It validates a parsed `staffing.json` against a documented schema with FOUR rules, each finding tagged with its `rule`: `schema` (top level is an object with `product` [non-empty str] + `iteration_budget` [int > 0] + `roles` [non-empty list of well-formed `{role,model,gate,done_criteria}` objects]), `bench_card` (every well-formed role name has a `<bench-dir>/<name>.md` card), `core_seat` (the five core seats product_manager/engineer/reviewer/qa_tester/release_gate are all staffed, in that fixed order), and `budget` (`iteration_budget` is a positive int, not a bool). Same shape as the rest of the linter family: a PURE `lint_manifest(data, bench_dir, manifest_path=...)` core returning frozen `ManifestFinding` / `ManifestLint` verdicts with `render()` / `to_dict()` and DETERMINISTIC finding order, plus a thin `lint_manifest_cli` I/O shell -- the MANIFEST-facing sibling of `doctor` (env), `lint-spec` (spec), `lint-config` (config) and `lint-bench` (bench). Purely additive and DORMANT: read-only, off the control path, nothing in the pipeline calls it, so item 19's manifest-driven pipeline got an enforceable contract before anything consumed it. 17 behaviors, fully offline.

- **iter 122 = the roadmap file split -- `PLATFORM_ROADMAP.md` compacted to a terse LIVE index plus this new TRACKED `PLATFORM_ROADMAP_ARCHIVE.md` (byte-for-byte move), with `ROADMAP_SIZE_WARN_CHARS` and TWO pure suite-enforced brakes.** RECOVERED in iteration 124: this bullet AND 122's ledger row were owed by iteration 123, which ended `ACTION: PENDING`, so 122's entire record was dropped and lived only in the subject of `efddc28`. The index had grown to 312,487 chars of which 88.8% was done history, and every roadmap-reading stage paid for it twice (read + rewrite); the 98 history bullets moved here VERBATIM, pinned by the sha256 `0fee18f4f5568de1404fe49786e1ec6a5648131fd2f698dabfa570b62dfaa904` over the lines matching `^- \*\*iter (\d+) ` in file order. Shipped `ROADMAP_SIZE_WARN_CHARS = 60000` plus two PURE, TOTAL verdict functions -- `roadmap_size_verdict(text) -> RoadmapSize` (frozen dataclass carrying `char_count`/`line_count`/`budget`/`over_budget`, budget read from the module global AT CALL TIME, strictly-greater-than boundary) and `roadmap_archive_gaps(index_text, archive_text)` (ledger numbers with no archive bullet; deliberately one-directional so archive-only history never fires) -- with the PEDAL deliberately a suite test rather than a CLI verb, since a read-only report nobody consults cannot change a decision. DORMANT: no call site on the control path, no CLI verb, no new artifact. Its own tester re-anchored the spec's TOTAL-count behaviors to the CLOSED FROZEN SET of 98 numbers so a correct future iteration could append a row without turning the brake RED -- the forward-stability fix that let this iteration's repair land at all.

- **iter 124 = the THIRD roadmap brake, walking from GIT SHIP-TRUTH back to the record -- plus the repair of the two records already lost and a flip of the written contract for who writes them.** Scout B candidate B1, redesigned: no grace window, and a record in EITHER file counts. The iter-122 pair guards only ONE direction (index size, and ledger -> archive), so both start from what the roadmap already claims and can only look where the ledger already points; nothing asked whether everything git says SHIPPED was recorded at all. Measured at HEAD before this ship: 99 `(foundry iter N)` ship tags in `git log` vs 98 ledger rows, with iterations 64 and 122 present in NO roadmap file -- while BOTH existing brakes read GREEN (index 47,915 chars = no warn; archive-gap list EMPTY). Root cause was the PROCESS: the contract deferred each iteration's record to the NEXT iteration's PM, so at a ~40% revert rate roughly one record in three was lost permanently, which for a product whose premise is fresh single-shot agents means silent, compounding amnesia in the only memory they have. Shipped three PURE, TOTAL functions -- `iteration_from_subject(subject)` (trailing `(foundry iter N)` tag only, matched against `rstrip()` and anchored at the END, zero-padding normalised, `None` for everything else), `shipped_iterations(subjects)` (ascending, de-duplicated `tuple`), `roadmap_ledger_gaps(index_text, archive_text, shipped)` (shipped numbers with NEITHER a `- iter N ` ledger row NOR a `- **iter N ` archive bullet, REUSING the existing two roadmap patterns, one-directional, `[]` on anything malformed) -- plus ONE new I/O seam `git_ship_subjects(repo_dir)` (`git log --format=%s`; returns `()` on non-zero exit, absent git, timeout or non-repository, so an unavailable git SKIPS the live check instead of failing a ship). Subjects are the ship-truth source because `products/*/state/` is gitignored, so no `final.md` survives a fresh clone. Repair shipped in the same commit: ledger rows for 122 and 124, archive bullets for 64, 122 and 124 -- iteration 64's record deliberately in the ARCHIVE ONLY, because iter 122's ledger oracle forbids a new row for an iteration <= 119 and its pinned hash selects only frozen numbers, so the recovery cannot weaken either. Contract flipped in `PLATFORM_ROADMAP.md` and `roles/pm.md`: each PM now writes its own row and bullet in its own ship commit. DORMANT (no call site, no CLI verb, no config field, no new artifact, `.gitignore` untouched, no restart needed); the PEDAL is the suite, which every stage and the post-release fresh clone already run.

- **iter 126 = the test gate learns the difference between an UNFINISHED tester CHECKPOINT and a genuinely RED suite, and spends the repair round on another TESTER round instead of a fix pass with nothing to fix.** Scout B candidate B1, re-scoped. MEASURED over the 194 tester artifacts in the fleet: of the 12 `RESULT: FAIL` verdicts, **10 are 386-981 byte checkpoints written by a round the agent CLI killed at its 600 s cap** and only 2 report a real red suite -- so the pipeline's most load-bearing gate signal is a false alarm ~83% of the time it fires. Correlating every placeholder-FAIL iteration with its `final.md` isolates the differentiator: `_platform` 115/117 and repolens 12/13 all PUSHED and all have a SUBSTANTIAL second tester report, while `_platform` 120/121/125 all REVERTED and their second report is itself a killed checkpoint (830/958/981 B). Not the code and not the suite -- 121 and 125 were both `3297 passed`, exit 0 -- only whether a tester round SURVIVED. Today such an iteration buys one `fix-tests` stage hunting failures that do not exist (iter 125's own `fix_tests.md`: "ZERO failing tests exist") plus one tester attempt; this spends the same wall-clock on TWO tester attempts and hands them a TRUTHFUL prompt. **What ships:** pure total `classify_test_report(text) -> PASS|UNFINISHED|RED|NONE` (an earned PASS outranks a checkpoint claim; reuses the anchored `parse_tester_result`, which had had NO control-path caller since it was written); the single I/O seam `read_test_disposition(path)`, whose `OSError -> "RED"` deliberately DEGRADES to the behavior that shipped before this iteration; and three module-level patchables `UNFINISHED_TEST_MARKER` = `PROGRESS: CHECKPOINT`, `UNFINISHED_TEST_RETRY_STAGES` = `(("tester-retry", "tester2.md"), ("tester-retry2", "tester3.md"))` and one shared `UNFINISHED_TEST_RETRY_PROMPT` so the two mirrored call sites can never tell the retry different stories. Wired at BOTH live orchestrators (`run_iteration`, `run_execution_plan`): the outer `contains(report, "RESULT: FAIL")` trigger is BYTE-UNCHANGED (verified as a diff CONTEXT line at both sites) and the RED branch is the old body re-indented and whitespace-normalised IDENTICAL, so the only new behavior lives inside a branch that could not previously exist. `roles/tester.md` duty 1 becomes FILE-FIRST and now MANDATES the marker for a round cut short while KEEPING the `RESULT:` sentinel -- which is why the pinned operator advice "never write RESULT: FAIL as a placeholder" is recorded here as HALF wrong: with no sentinel the report never reaches the repair branch at all and the iteration is strictly worse off. **Test routing was itself the fix for iterations 121 and 125** (same feature, same green suite, same revert, because a spec gave the isolated tester 100% of the coverage and that one stage was killed 6 times): the ENGINEER owns `tests/test_iter126_helpers.py` (35 unit tests, behaviors 1-6) and the TESTER owns `tests/test_iter126_behavior.py` (36 tests, behaviors 7-12), 15 of them a single `parametrize` over both orchestrators so a one-site implementation fails immediately. Suite 3297 -> **3368 = +71**, exactly the count the two new files collect. **Gate control test:** forcing `read_test_disposition` back to its pre-126 answer fails 18 of the tester's 36 -- the new tests are wired to the feature, not passing for the wrong reason. **Falsified and de-listed here so nothing re-derives them:** per-product `fast_test_cmd` (no product in the fleet has a slow suite); the operator's "preserve partial stage output" item (the agent CLI self-caps BELOW `STAGE_TIMEOUT`, so the child exits normally and the 48-byte log already IS its complete stdout -- residual one-liner: the `TimeoutExpired` handler discards `e.stdout`/`e.stderr`); and swapping the outer trigger for an anchored predicate with a "no verdict -> repair" fail-safe, which would turn existing routing tests RED because `_make_run_stage` defaults an unscripted report to the empty string. **Known and deliberate:** the marker-only UNFINISHED class is unreachable from either live path (no sentinel, no branch) and exists only as a unit test; and after both retries are exhausted the run proceeds to `final` with ZERO reverts, leaving all revert authority at the gate. **Hazard this raises, worth a card line:** the outer trigger is an unanchored substring check on the tester's own report, so a report that QUOTES the sentinel while discussing it routes itself into the repair branch -- reference both tokens descriptively, never verbatim. Ship set = {foundry.py, ARCHITECTURE.md (new stage row 4c), roles/tester.md, DIRECTIONS.md, both roadmap files, 2 new test files}. Records written by the release gate (see the STATUS note in the index).
- **iter 127 -- ANCHOR THE OUTER TEST-GATE TRIGGER: only an EARNED tester PASS skips the repair round.** Iteration 126 classified the tester report INSIDE the trigger and deliberately left the trigger itself an unanchored `contains(report, <fail sentinel>)` substring scan. Measured this iteration over all 198 `products/*/state/iter-*/tester*.md` artifacts with `classify_test_report`: 179 PASS with no mention of the token, **6 PASS that MENTION it in prose -> a FALSE fire**, 8 RED, 5 UNFINISHED, 0 with no verdict. So 6 of the 19 fires (32%) burned a `fix-tests` + `tester-rerun` pair on a round that had already passed (`_platform` iters 117, 118, 70 x2, 73; `repolens` iter 12), each pair costing two rounds under the ~600 s cap and overwriting the report the release gate then reads. FIX = one pure module-level `needs_test_repair(disposition)` + patchable `TEST_GATE_REPAIR_DISPOSITIONS = ("UNFINISHED", "RED")`, routing BOTH twins (`run_iteration`, `run_execution_plan`) on `read_test_disposition` alone; the test gate stops calling `contains` while the review/release gates keep it. SECOND WIN: a checkpoint carrying the marker but NO fail sentinel -- the class iteration 126 recorded as "unreachable from either live path" -- now buys a tester retry instead of sailing to a certain revert, which is exactly the class that lost iterations 121 and 125. **De-listing honoured, not re-proposed:** iteration 126 falsified the variant with a `no verdict -> repair` fail-safe (every pre-127 driver writes an unscripted report as the empty string, which classifies `NONE`, so that variant turns existing routing tests RED). Behavior 8 keeps `NONE` OUT of the repair set, re-derived independently before reading that bullet; only the anchored-trigger half ships. Deliberate behavior deltas beyond the false-alarm fix: a missing/unreadable report now takes the RED path (fail-closed; `contains` swallowed the OSError), and a mid-file fail sentinel with no final verdict no longer triggers (zero such artifacts in the 198). NOT changed: the classifier trio, the retry stage tuple and prompt, gate keying/revert messages, config, CLI, sentinels. **Role cards untouched ON PURPOSE** -- the live loop runs the pre-127 module in memory, so relaxing the tester's prose rule in this same iteration would false-trigger this very iteration; it is queued as the successor. Ship set = {foundry.py, tests/test_iter127_behavior.py, both roadmap files}.
- **iter 128 -- THE PRODUCT CONFIG FAILS CLOSED ON AN UNKNOWN KEY, naming the offending key and its nearest known field.** Scout A candidate A1, and roadmap NEXT UP item (a). The adopter-authored config JSON is the primary integration surface of a framework whose vision sentence is "point it at any git repo", and `load_config` filtered it to known dataclass fields, so a misspelled key was silently a DEFAULT. MEASURED LIVE, not read from source: `"push_enable": false` loaded to `cfg.push_enabled == True`, i.e. it silently PUSHED against the adopter's explicit intent, and `"test_command": "echo hi"` loaded to `cfg.test_cmd == "uv run pytest"`, i.e. it silently ran a DIFFERENT quality gate than the adopter believed. `lint_config` advertises catching configs that "silently defeat the push guard" but takes an already-parsed `ProductConfig`, so it runs AFTER the drop and structurally cannot ever see this class. **Why FAIL-CLOSED rather than the softer raise-on-near-miss / warn-on-the-rest split:** `difflib.get_close_matches(cutoff=0.7)` over the 19 field names resolves `push_enable -> push_enabled` and `test_command -> test_cmd` but returns NOTHING for the bare key `"push"`, arguably the likeliest typo of all -- under a warn-on-non-near-miss design that key would still silently push, so the suggestion is a MESSAGE-QUALITY feature and never the trigger. **Precedent that makes the raise consistent rather than a new hazard class:** of the 19 fields exactly 3 lack a default (`name`, `repo`, `allowed_push_repo`) and dropping `allowed_push_repo` ALREADY raised `TypeError`, so `load_config` already failed closed on a malformed config and the dispatcher's skip-and-log path for that raise was already exercised. **Blast radius, measured: provably inert against today's fleet** -- all four live product configs (`_platform` 13 keys, `proactive-loop-agent` 14, `repolens` 13, `resilient-agent-loop-primitives` 13) carry ZERO unknown keys, so the guard can only fire on a future mistake; and `dispatcher.py:116` already wraps the load in `try/except Exception`, logs the exception repr and `continue`s, so one bad config skips exactly one team for one round and never aborts the company -- which is also why the MESSAGE TEXT is the deliverable, since the operator reads that repr in DISPATCH_LOG rather than the file they mistyped. Shipped: pure `unknown_config_keys(raw)` (known set derived by reflecting `dataclasses.fields(ProductConfig)` AT CALL TIME so it can never drift from the schema) exempting every `_`-prefixed key, because the repo's own `foundry.config.json` and `foundry.config.example.json` use `_comment`/`_note` as a documented comment convention an adopter will copy; pure `suggest_config_key(key)`; `ConfigKeyError(ValueError)` so the dispatcher's existing `except Exception` catches it with NO dispatcher change; and ONE guarded call site in `load_config`, placed AHEAD of its two `mkdir` calls so a rejected config leaves no `work_root`/`state` directory behind. **The contract this INVERTS, enumerated in the spec rather than discovered during a red run:** four existing tests deliberately pinned "an unknown key is ignored" -- `tests/test_foundry.py::test_unknown_keys_ignored`, `tests/test_iter02_behavior.py::test_b1_unknown_keys_still_ignored`, and `tests/test_iter81_behavior.py`'s `test_b06_unknown_key_filtered_no_error` + `test_b06_unknown_key_with_optin_together`, the latter pair under a section header reading "regression: the known-field filter is intact". The complete list was measured, not grepped, by patching `foundry.load_config` under a session-scoped pytest plugin and running the whole suite, which found exactly 4 real load sites carrying unknown keys and no others; each was UPDATED to assert the raise and RENAMED so no test name or comment lies, while `test_b1_shipped_platform_config_loads_with_defaults` (which loads the REAL `_platform` config) stayed green untouched and is now also a live fleet-inertness guard. **Anti-stranding test split**, as iterations 126 and 127 did: the ENGINEER owned `tests/test_iter128_helpers.py` (behaviors 1-3, the two pure functions) and the TESTER owned `tests/test_iter128_behavior.py` (behaviors 4-10, the `load_config` call site), so one killed stage could not strand the iteration. **Deliberately OUT of scope and recorded so nothing re-derives them:** `dispatcher.py` was NOT touched (26 existing suite guards freeze it byte-for-byte, so any dispatcher edit turns the suite RED -- which is also why the guard had to live in `load_config`, where the dispatcher inherits it for free); no `strict=` opt-out kwarg, since a lenient mode with no production consumer is dead config surface; the staffing-manifest path is excluded, because `products/repolens/staffing.json` is a DIFFERENT schema whose 3 keys are all "unknown" to `ProductConfig`; and the dispatcher roster configs are parsed by `parse_dispatch_work_items`, never by `load_config`. The SECOND consumer (`lint_config_cli` re-reading the raw dict at the config path to emit a `ConfigFinding`) is queued as NEXT UP item (b). Baseline suite measured before the change: 3479 passed in 37.85 s.

- **iter 129 -- THE RETRY DELAY NOW READS THE FAILURE KIND: a stage killed by the ~600 s agent-CLI cap retries after 60 s instead of 10 minutes, while a genuinely busy backend keeps every second of its existing backoff.** Scout B candidate B1, picked over Scout A's A1 (collapse the nine `company_*_cli` clones onto one generic driver) because A1 changes zero observable behavior -- its whole value is ~180 executable lines of source -- whereas this changes a DECISION on the live control path and its value is measured machine time. **The defect, read from source:** `run_stage` chose its delay purely by attempt index (`delay = BACKOFFS[min(attempt - 1, len(BACKOFFS) - 1)]`, foundry.py:12230) and never looked at the failure it was reacting to, even though the failure text was already in hand -- `blob` is the same variable logged one line above at 12228 -- so a cap timeout and a "the service is busy" error were priced identically. **MEASURED on the live `dispatcher.out` window (08-03 09:41 -> 08-07 06:23 = 92.71 h, 3 teams, concurrency 1), not read from source:** the company slept 49,800 s in backoffs = 13.83 h = 14.9% of ALL uptime, and clustering the 57 `tail:` fields the dispatcher already logs puts 55 of them into exactly four failure shapes (the other 2 are ordinary agent narration from a stage that talked but never wrote its output file, and fall to the safe `other` default, i.e. today's `BACKOFFS` ladder) -- `agent run timed out after 600s` x35, `The service is busy` (with either `unexpected error during processing` or an upstream `internal error: Internal server error`) x10, `Connection stalled -- no data received for 120 s` x9, and the agent-CLI help text `the native shortcut did not match -- check syntax` x1. Pairing each failure with the sleep it ACTUALLY paid (rather than counting failures, which hides the cost) shows 33,600 s of that sleep followed a kind where waiting cannot possibly help: cap timeouts sat at attempt positions 1/2/3 seventeen, seven and six times = 17x600 + 7x1200 + 6x2400 = 33,000 s, plus 600 s for the one instant-fail. On the new ladder those become 17x60 + 7x120 + 6x240 = 3,300 s and 60 s, so **30,240 s = 8.4 h = 9.1% of company uptime is reclaimed (~2.3 h/day, roughly 2.5 lost `_platform` iterations per day)** while the 10 genuine service failures are untouched. For scale, the 40-minute tail alone bought exactly 2 successful stage-runs out of 547 for 8.33 h of sleep. Shipped: pure total case-insensitive `classify_attempt_failure(blob) -> str` over a patchable marker table returning one of `timeout` / `cli-error` / `service` / `stalled` / `other`; pure `retry_delay(kind, attempt) -> int`; module-level `RETRY_DELAY_FLOOR = 60`, `TIMEOUT_BACKOFFS = [60, 120, 240]`, `FAST_RETRY_KINDS = ("timeout", "cli-error")`; and ONE changed call site, with both functions referenced BY BARE NAME so the module globals resolve at CALL time and a `monkeypatch.setattr` bites. **Precedence is deliberately CONSERVATIVE-FIRST and order-independent:** a blob carrying both a service marker and a timeout marker classifies as `service` regardless of which appears first in the text, because mis-pricing a struggling backend as a cap timeout is the single error that could make things worse, so every ambiguity lands on the LONG ladder. The fast ladder ESCALATES (60/120/240) rather than pinning a flat 60 s, so a persistently timing-out stage still decays, and a floor plus an empty-ladder fallback keep `retry_delay` total. **Why `BACKOFFS` itself was NOT edited** (which is what Scout B's B3, a strict subset, would have done and why B3 was explicitly not also picked): `tests/test_iter119_behavior.py:643` pins `BACKOFFS == [600, 1200, 2400]`, so a separate constant keeps that regression green and makes the diff strictly additive to the existing ladder. **Deliberately out of scope, recorded so nothing re-derives it:** `MAX_ATTEMPTS`, `COOLDOWNS` and `sleep_interruptible` untouched (infra cooldowns measured a non-issue -- 9 infra-fails, 0.00 h of gap, because under the dispatcher the next team takes the shift immediately and those cooldowns only bind single-team `run_forever`); no new `foundry` subcommand, since the deliverable is the changed decision and a read-only CLI would be the operator-criticised "brake with no pedal"; and the `stalled` kind stays on the long ladder pending measurement, queued as NEXT UP (e). **The restart caveat, stated up front so it is never reported as "the fix did not work":** `dispatcher.py` does `import foundry` ONCE (line 41) and calls `run_iteration` inside its loop, so every module-level constant and function body is pinned in memory for the dispatcher's whole lifetime and this change is INERT until an operator restart -- which is also precisely why it is resume-safe, since no in-flight loop switches semantics mid-shift. Observability: the backoff log line keeps the substring `backing off` (so `classify_event` still stamps it `backoff`, per `tests/test_iter26_behavior.py:112`) and now also names the classified kind. **Public-safety constraint discovered by RUNNING the guard rather than by review:** the committed base64 `scripts/leak_denylist.txt` denies the model-vendor name, and this archive bullet initially quoted it verbatim from the measured log tails, which turned `tests/test_iter122_behavior.py::test_b11_archive_scans_clean_under_the_committed_denylist` RED. The vendor name therefore must not enter code, tests or docs; the generic marker `service is busy` was present in ALL 10 measured service failures, so the classifier loses nothing by never naming a vendor. Baseline suite measured before the change: 3519 passed in 37.25 s.

- **iter 130 -- "SHIPPED" IS NOT "LIVE": a read-only `live-lag` report plus one `doctor` WARN names the iterations git reports as shipped that the RUNNING brain cannot be executing.** Scout B candidate B3, picked over Scout A's candidate A (give the `stalled` failure kind its own retry ladder) on two independent grounds. First the role card's diversity guard: A is the same function (`retry_delay`), the same lens and the same kind-to-delay shape as iteration 129, one of the last two shipped features, and viable candidates from the other lens existed -- Scout A raised that objection against itself. Second, and decisive, is the very defect this iteration exposes: iteration 129's repricing IS NOT RUNNING, so A would have extended a ladder the live brain does not use and reclaimed zero measured seconds until a restart. **Measured this run, from two sources, and the two agree exactly:** the dispatcher log holds 5 `dispatcher up` banners (`08-03 09:41:14`, `08-03 22:08:08`, `08-04 00:26:18`, `08-04 13:27:26`, `08-05 15:13:46`) and the LAST one matches the live dispatcher process's real start time to the second, which is what makes a last-banner parse ground truth rather than a guess. Against that instant, iterations 118, 119, 122, 124, 126, 127, 128 and 129 all landed on main AFTER the brain launched -- and `dispatcher.py` does a plain `import foundry` (line 41) then calls `foundry.run_iteration` in-process (line 129) with no `importlib.reload` and no self-restart anywhere in the file, so every module-level constant and function body is pinned in memory for the process's whole lifetime. Eight consecutive iterations of verified, pushed work are therefore INERT: the learnings-digest character cap (118), the attempt-aware retry directive (119), both roadmap ledger brakes (122, 124), the test-gate disposition and its anchored trigger (126, 127), fail-closed config (128) and the failure-kind retry repricing (129). Confirmed independently from the prompt of this very stage, which received a 910-character pinned-head bullet whole where post-118 code caps a bullet at 800. **Why this is a decision and not a report:** the roadmap index, the archive and the decision log all say "shipped" and none of them distinguishes live from inert, so the operator had no way to learn that a restart was owed; the pedal is therefore the `doctor` WARN, the surface the operator already consults, not the CLI verb, per the standing rule that a read-only CLI is not the deliverable and the CHANGED DECISION is. It is the same class as iteration 124's ship-truth brake (the roadmap claiming more than git supports) one level deeper: the CODE claiming more than the PROCESS supports. **Shape, following the additive-dormant pattern of iterations 117 and 124:** pure total `parse_brain_launch(log_text, year=)` taking the LAST banner by POSITION IN THE TEXT (never by stamp ordering) and returning `None` on absent-or-malformed rather than guessing -- the year is an explicit caller argument because the log stamp carries `MM-DD HH:MM:SS` and NO year; pure total `inert_iterations(launch_epoch, commits)` treating an equal epoch as LIVE and returning `()` for an unknown launch instant so the check can never fire falsely; the one new I/O seam `git_ship_commits(repo)` returning `()` on every failure mode and reusing the shipped pure `iteration_from_subject`; a `live_lag_line(cfg)` composer that distinguishes UNKNOWN from HEALTHY; a `live-lag` verb on the shipped 0/2 "there is something to report" exit convention so it never gates a build; and exactly ONE new line inside `doctor`, sourced from the same composer so there is a single source of truth. **Deliberately OUT of scope and recorded so nothing re-derives it:** any automatic restart, `importlib.reload` or self-healing of the running brain, which would touch resume semantics and is forbidden by the quality bar -- acting on the lag stays a human decision; and pid discovery or `ps` parsing, since the log's last banner was validated against the real process and needs no process table. **The self-referential caveat, stated up front so it is never reported as "the fix did not work":** this iteration is itself inert until the operator restarts, so a live brain will keep reporting itself as lagging -- which is exactly the correct nudge, and is also why the change is resume-safe, since no in-flight loop switches semantics mid-shift.

- **iter 131 -- the decision log learns the candidate-heading shapes scouts actually write (roadmap item (g), candidates half).** `parse_scout_candidates` admitted only a line starting `## Candidate` (case-insensitive), so a scout that lettered its headings id-first (`## A1 --`, `## B2`, `## C1 (primary)` with an em-dash, `## H1`, `## I3`) vanished from `DIRECTIONS.md` with no sign of the loss. MEASURED BY THE PM LEAD THIS RUN over all 136 scout slates on disk, not recalled: 32 of 136 (24%) parsed to ZERO candidates; under the union matcher 0 do, 33 slates render more, and a superset assertion over all 136 files proves no currently-parsed candidate is lost. In the COMMITTED artifact (`git show HEAD:DIRECTIONS.md`) 5 of 27 blocks improve -- iter-111 0->6, iter-118 0->6, iter-129 0->6, iter-116 3->6, iter-120 3->6 -- and because `refresh_directions_file` regenerates the whole file from the state dirs on every scouted iteration, the repair is RETROACTIVE rather than forward-only. **The scout's own sketch was WRONG and the measurement is what caught it:** it proposed REPLACING the rule with an id-first pattern ('an optional `Candidate` prefix, any 1-2 letter id + digits'), which drops the DIGIT-LESS ids used by iterations 104, 105, 110, 121 and 130 -- iter-130 would fall 6->3 and iter-121 6->0. The shipped rule is therefore a UNION of two branches, never a replacement. False-positive risk was closed two-sided before speccing: the id-first branch admits 99 additional headings across the company and every one is a genuine candidate heading, while matching ZERO of the 626 distinct non-candidate `##` headings; the six decoys the tests assert negatively (`## Measured grounding`, `## Note for the PM lead`, `## Considered, measured, and deliberately NOT proposed`, `## Ranking`, `## Diversity note`, `## Deliberately NOT proposed`) are all taken verbatim from real slates. **Deliberately OUT of scope, recorded so nothing re-derives it:** the `parse_triage_winner` half (21 `pm.md` files carry a `## Triage` heading and still yield `None`) is the DECLARED NEXT BITE, because widening two parsers plus a card in one slice is what turns a small iteration into a reverted one; also out, rendering 'present but unparsed' distinctly from 'absent', and any edit to `refresh_directions_file` or `run_iteration`. **The self-referential caveat, stated up front so it is never reported as 'the fix did not work':** the running brain imported `foundry` before this lands (iteration 130's live-lag finding), so this ship's own `DIRECTIONS.md` is still generated by the OLD parser -- the 6 healed blocks appear on the first scouted iteration AFTER the operator restarts the dispatcher. That is also why the change is resume-safe: no in-flight loop switches semantics mid-shift.

- **iter 132 -- the freeze-guard meta-test stops policing ONE FUNCTION NAME and starts policing GUARD BEHAVIOR (roadmap item (a), the top open item).** Scout B candidate B1, picked over Scout A's A1 (a phrase-anchored, cross-validated fallback for `parse_triage_winner`) on the role card's diversity guard: A1 is the same file, the same builder and self-describedly the same union-with-precedence shape as iteration 131, one of the last two shipped features, while a viable other-lens candidate existed. `tests/test_control_path_freeze_scope.py` selected the guards it polices with `node.name == GUARD_NAME`, one literal string, so a permanent freeze guard under any other name was invisible to the test written to prevent exactly the iter-83/84 regression that reverted iter-85 (a guard freezing `README.md`/`roles/` blocks every later docs-touching or role-touching iteration). **MEASURED BY THE PM LEAD THIS RUN over 4,383 test functions, never inherited from a prior spec:** the exact-name selector sees 12 guards; the behavior selector sees 26; so 14 real every-suite freeze guards across 9 further function names (`test_b10_control_path_files_byte_unchanged`, `test_ac_guard_and_control_path_byte_unchanged`, `test_b8_non_prose_files_byte_unchanged`, `test_b8/b9_control_path_and_guard_scripts_byte_unchanged`, `test_b8/b11_control_path_byte_unchanged_from_head`, `test_b11/b12_control_path_byte_unchanged`) in files `test_iter50` through `test_iter72` were unpoliced. The change is provably suite-green on landing rather than hopefully so: of the 26, ZERO freeze `README.md` or `roles/` and 26 of 26 contain `dispatcher.py`, so both the negative and the positive existing assertion hold under the wider population. Two figures the roadmap carried are CORRECTED here: the distinct pathspec sets are **4**, not 3 (`{dispatcher.py, scripts/}` x10, `{.gitignore, dispatcher.py, scripts/}` x9, `{dispatcher.py, scripts/leak_denylist.txt, scripts/leak_guard.py}` x6, `{dispatcher.py}` x1), and the 10 distinct names are 1 policed plus 9 invisible. **The matcher SHAPE is a measured decision, not a style choice.** Three variants all select the same 26 today, so recall cannot separate them, and precision must: a function whose ONLY string is a docstring reading "we deliberately do not run git diff --quiet HEAD -- dispatcher.py here" is selected by the blob-substring variant and by the contiguous-phrase variant, and is correctly REJECTED by the shipped element-form rule (each token must be a standalone string constant). That closes the scout's own strongest objection -- a too-loose selector picks up a test that merely MENTIONS the command, which the third assertion then forces to freeze `dispatcher.py`, manufacturing a red suite from a healthy test. Recall was cross-checked in the other direction too: of 45 functions whose NAME contains `byte_unchanged` or `control_path`, the 19 not selected are absence/off-control-path assertions and the meta-test's own helpers, and none of them invokes git. **Non-vacuity is a FLOOR (>= 26), deliberately not an equality.** Iteration 119's `test_ac_no_byte_unchanged_freeze_guard_added` scans only its OWN file, so nothing repo-wide forbids a future iteration from adding a legitimate guard, and an equality assertion would turn the suite red and revert that iteration for a bookkeeping reason -- the same docs-only-revert trap the roadmap DEADLINE note warns about. A floor still catches the failure mode that matters (a selector that matches nothing or regresses to the old population), the superset assertion catches a selector that loses a currently-policed guard, and the prose-only rejection test catches loosening -- so precision is tested as a RULE rather than pinned as a count. **Deliberately OUT of scope, recorded so nothing re-derives it:** the optional 25-guard CONSOLIDATION (much larger, and it does not unblock strangler steps 2/3 since one guard must keep freezing `dispatcher.py`); widening the FORBIDDEN check itself from element membership to whitespace-tokenised matching, which would catch a pathspec written as ONE joined string but risks flagging a guard whose assert message names `README.md` in prose -- all 26 live guards use list-element literals, so this is a real but unfired residual, as is a future guard that builds its pathspec from a module-level constant or a shared helper (AST string constants inside the function body cannot see it: this narrows the hole, it does not close it); and any `foundry.py` change. **No self-referential inertness caveat applies, unlike iterations 130 and 131:** this is a suite test, not brain code, so it takes effect for the very next gate run with no dispatcher restart owed -- and being test-only with no control-flow change, it cannot alter resume semantics for an in-flight loop.

- **iter 133 -- every lens the live rotation can assign is now DEFINED in the scout role card, and a suite brake keeps it that way (roadmap item (f)).** Scout A candidate A2, picked over A3 (name the `auth` failure kind, give it its own retry ladder, write a per-product `STOP`) on two grounds the PM lead measured this run rather than recalled. First, EFFECT THIS SHIFT: `build_prompt` passes only the role-card PATH into a stage prompt, so a stage agent reads `roles/*.md` FROM DISK at stage time, while the long-running dispatcher holds the imported `foundry.py` in memory -- a card edit therefore changes the very next scout stage with no restart owed, whereas A1 and A3 both live in `foundry.py` and would have been inert until an operator restart (iteration 130's live-lag finding). Second, BLAST RADIUS: A3's shipping half writes a product-halting sentinel from a rule derived from 18 attempt logs in one product and its conservative half multiplies a live retry sleep by 10x, while A2 cannot break the running loop at all. A1 (`parse_triage_winner` precedence) was declined by the diversity guard as the sibling parser of iteration 131's `parse_scout_candidates`, shipped one iteration earlier into the same `DIRECTIONS.md` artifact; it stays roadmap item (g) with its 49/4/0 replay preserved. **THE DRIFT, MEASURED:** `PM_SCOUT_LENS_POOL` has held SIX lenses since iteration 113 wired `select_scout_lenses(iteration)` into `run_iteration`, but the scout card defined exactly TWO and then instructed the scout not to propose candidates "from the other lens" -- a sentence that presumes a two-lens world. So four of six lenses reached a live scout as a bare NAME with no instruction, and only one iteration in six briefed a fully documented pair. The harm reached the deciding stage of this very iteration: `roles/pm.md` told its own PM that `pm_scout_a.md` was the "(new-capability lens)" slate when scout A had actually run `hardening/DX` and scout B `integration-and-adoption`, so the PM was handed a false premise about the two artifacts it exists to triage, by its own prompt. `ARCHITECTURE.md` and `docs/DUAL_PM_SCOUT_SPEC.md` asserted the same retired mapping, and no test bound the pool to the card, so the drift was unbounded. **WHAT SHIPPED:** the four missing lenses (`integration-and-adoption`, `simplification-and-deletion`, `performance-and-throughput`, `narrative-and-docs`) are defined in the card's lens section as instruction a scout can generate from, not restatements of their own names; the two-lens sentence is generalized to the single ASSIGNED lens; the retired a/b mapping is replaced in all three tracked docs by the fact that the pair is ROTATED per iteration by `select_scout_lenses` over the six-entry pool; the stale comment above the pool claiming ZERO orchestrator call site (with wiring as "the pre-declared next bite") is corrected to record iteration 113's live wiring; and a new pure, total, additive-DORMANT `scout_lens_audit(pool, card_text)` returns a frozen `LensDocAudit` of `defined` / `undocumented` / `orphaned` / `ok`. **THE DELIVERABLE IS THE ASSERTION, NOT THE FUNCTION**, and deliberately NOT a CLI verb: the operator's standing rule is that a read-only report nobody consults changes no decision, so the consumer here is a SUITE test that fails the ship when the real pool and the real card disagree -- the complement of iteration 107's exact-6-tuple assertion on the pool, and what makes adding a seventh lens undocumented impossible. Parsing is deliberately narrow so prose cannot silently document a lens: definitions are read only between the lens heading and the next `## ` heading, and a quoted name must be followed by the ` -- ` separator, so `- "hardening/DX" is one` is prose. **Deliberately OUT of scope, recorded so nothing re-derives it:** adding, removing or reordering any pool entry, any change to `select_scout_lenses`, `scout_phase_outcome`, `build_prompt`, `run_stage` or the stage sequence, the `auth` failure kind and its ladder (A3), the `parse_triage_winner` half (item (g)), and wholesale compaction of the roadmap index. **No inertness caveat applies to the half that matters:** the card and doc edits are read from disk per stage, so they brief the NEXT scout with no dispatcher restart; the new function is dormant with zero pipeline call site, so an in-flight loop's resume semantics are byte-identical.
- **iter 134 -- `foundry lint-config` reports a typo'd product-config key as a structured FINDING with exit 1, instead of "cannot read config" with exit 2 (roadmap item (b)).** Scout A candidate A1, picked over scout B's B1 (collapse the nine near-identical `company_*_cli` roll-up bodies, a measured -250 to -350 lines). The novelty block read `verdict=VARIED`, so no rut had to be broken; the pick was made on value-per-blast-radius. A1 is the roadmap's own top open item and its diff is one `except`-branch split plus one pure function, while B1 would be this product's first non-additive refactor of shipped code, rewriting NINE CLI contracts at once across 547 lines with fourteen existing test modules as its only oracle -- the exact diff shape that has historically blown the ~600 s stage cap here (this product's engineer stage sat at a 600 s median through iterations 109-110). B1 is therefore RECORDED as new roadmap item (i) rather than declined. **THE DEFECT, MEASURED:** iteration 128's fail-closed unknown-key guard (`unknown_config_keys` / `suggest_config_key` / `describe_config_key`) had exactly ONE consumer, the `load_config` raise -- scout A confirmed no other call site outside its own two test modules. Because `load_config` raises BEFORE a `ProductConfig` exists, and `lint_config(cfg)` takes an already-parsed config, the linter structurally could not see the one class of mistake it advertises: scout A ran the real CLI over three scratch configs and a `push_enable` typo exited **2** ('cannot read config'), the code its own docstring reserves for an unreadable or invalid-JSON FILE, while `1` means 'config-errors'. So a launch wrapper or CI step could not distinguish a one-character fixable typo from a missing or corrupt file, and `--json` emitted `{config_path, error: <one prose blob>, exit_code: 2}` with NO `findings` array, leaving the offending key recoverable only by regexing English out of an exception repr. **WHAT SHIPPED:** a new pure module-level `config_key_findings(raw)` returning one error-level `ConfigFinding` per key `unknown_config_keys` reports, in ascending key order, with the offending KEY in the machine-readable `field` slot and the `suggest_config_key` hint in `detail` (detail always opens with the literal `unknown config key`, and omits any `did you mean` clause when there is no close match, so the message never asserts a suggestion it does not have); plus a split of `lint_config_cli`'s `except` branch on `ConfigKeyError` ONLY, which re-reads the raw dict and renders those findings through the SAME `ConfigLint.render()` / `to_dict()` / exit-code path as any other lint, so text, JSON and exit code cannot drift. **TWO MEASUREMENTS DECIDED THE DESIGN, and both were run before the spec was written.** First, the exit-code flip is safe: the only exit-2 assertions in the suite are for a MISSING file and for INVALID JSON (`tests/test_iter60_behavior.py`), and NOTHING asserts 2 for an unknown key -- so the flip is a strict narrowing of a generic `except`, not a broken contract. Second, the schema must NOT grow: `tests/test_iter60_behavior.py:293` pins every finding dict to exactly `("field", "level", "detail")`, so scout A's suggested structured `suggestion` field would have reddened the suite -- the hint belongs in `detail`, and the `field` slot is what makes the KEY machine-readable at zero schema cost. **Deliberately OUT of scope, recorded so nothing re-derives it:** the `company-lint-config` roll-up, which reaches products through the `load_config` + `gather_config_lint` seams and NOT through `lint_config_cli`, so a typo'd team still lands in its `errors` list as an ERRORED team rather than as a LINTED team carrying a config finding -- a real follow-up, but it edits the nine-way-duplicated body that item (i) proposes to collapse, and doing both at once is the oversized version of this work; any change to the `ConfigFinding` / `ConfigLint` schema; any relaxation of the fail-closed `load_config` guard; and merging an unknown-key report WITH the normal field lint, which is impossible while `load_config` raises first -- the unknown-key findings REPLACE the field lint for that run, and the spec states that limitation rather than pretending otherwise. A fail-safe seam keeps the change from ever making the old path worse: `config_key_findings` is called by BARE MODULE NAME, and if it returns nothing or raises, the CLI falls back to today's byte-identical exit-2 message. **THE CENSUS ITEM (i) POINTS AT, so its future engineer does not re-derive it:** scout B enumerated every `monkeypatch.setattr` target across the fourteen test modules covering the `company-*` verbs, and they land on exactly the two levels a collapse preserves -- `load_config` (48 patches across 9 modules, called by bare name inside the shared body) and each verb's OWN `gather_*` (8/8/7/6/5/5/4/4/4 patches, resolved as a module global inside the thin wrapper at call time). NOT ONE test patches a `summarize_company_*` seam; the `summarize_*` patches that exist name the single-product layer (`summarize_history`, `summarize_timing`, `summarize_weak_tests`, `summarize_events`), which a collapse does not touch. Eight modules also patch the `company_*_cli` NAMES themselves (3 patches each) to assert `main()` routing, which is precisely why all nine public names must survive verbatim. The one trap left: `summarize_company` (the `status` member) does NOT follow the `summarize_company_<stem>` rule, so a name-derived or stem-built dispatch silently misses it -- pass the callables explicitly. Scout B also proved, by an AST scan of all 210 module-level `foundry.py` functions against every reference in the repo, that the zero-reference set is EMPTY: there is no dead code in this tree, so the deletion lens has to target near-identical LIVE paths, not orphans.

- **iter 135 -- the `"stalled"` failure kind gets its OWN retry ladder through a per-kind `KIND_RETRY_LADDERS` map (roadmap item (e), recorded there as "the top ready-to-ship item").** Scout B candidate B2, picked over scout A's A1 (collapse the `CompanyWeakTests`/`CompanyConstantAsserts`/`CompanySkippedTests` triplet onto a shared frozen base, a measured -288 lines proven by three EXACTLY equal 89-line non-docstring bodies). The novelty block read `verdict=VARIED`, so no rut had to be broken; the pick was value-per-blast-radius. A1's evidence is the best-measured deletion case this product has produced and it stays on the board, but it rewrites 523 lines of shipped code in one contiguous NON-additive diff -- the same shape iteration 134 declined as item (i) -- against an `engineer` stage whose attempt duration already sits at a **median 600s with 11 timeouts** on a hard 600s cap. B2's diff is one commented dict plus a two-line precedence branch in a pure function, and it buys measured THROUGHPUT rather than measured tidiness. **Scout B's own #1 (B1, parallelize the quality-check command, a measured 46.84s -> 16.76s at `-n auto`) was declined on a structural conflict the scout itself surfaced: its author says the ship "must repeat the parallel run several times" and that `-n 4` was never measured, while the operator's pinned tester rule allows at most ONE full-suite run per testing stage** -- a change that cannot be validated inside the pipeline that must validate it is not a small safe increment, and it fails every gate CLOSED if `pytest-xdist` is ever absent. B3 was, by its own admission, a third consecutive visit to the learnings-digest function. **THE DEFERRAL THIS REVERSES, AND THE MEASUREMENT THAT DECIDED IT.** Iteration 129 made the retry delay failure-kind-aware but left `stalled` on the long `BACKOFFS = [600, 1200, 2400]` ladder ON PURPOSE, because the evidence that a stall is a transient LOCAL condition rather than a network fault was "suggestive but unmeasured" -- and naming it as its own kind is what made the question decidable. It is now decided three ways, all re-measured on the CURRENT log rather than inherited: (1) DISTRIBUTION -- 16 stalled attempts across 9 stage-runs, and 9 of 9 stage-runs are test-RUNNING stages (6 `tester`, 1 `tester-rerun`, 1 `fix-tests`, plus one more tester) with ZERO in `pm`, `pm_scout_a/b`, `engineer`, `reviewer` or `final`; a network fault would spread across stages roughly in proportion to attempts, so this is independent corroboration of the operator's pinned tester rule; (2) THE LONG LADDER RESCUES NOBODY -- 7 of 9 stage-runs never reached attempt 4 (so they never needed a long wait) while the 2 that exhausted stalled on all four attempts DESPITE paying 4,200s each; (3) THE SLEEP IS FLEET-WIDE -- `dispatcher.py` is a single-threaded round-robin and the delay is an in-process `time.sleep` on the attempt loop, so the 13,800s (3.83h) blocked all three products, not just the stalled one. The roadmap had priced this as one team's waste; it is the fleet's. **WHY `[60, 300, 1200]` AND NOT THE FAST LADDER, arithmetic the PM lead reconstructed rather than assumed.** The 16 attempts decompose as 2 exhausted runs (3 sleeps each: 600+1200+2400 = 4,200s) + 6 runs paying one 600s sleep + 1 run paying 600+1200 -- which sums to EXACTLY the measured 13,800s, so the reconstruction is confirmed, not asserted. Under the shipped ladder that population pays 3,840s, reclaiming 9,960s (2.77h, a 72% cut). The pure `TIMEOUT_BACKOFFS = [60, 120, 240]` would reclaim 3.45h instead, and that extra 0.68h was DELIBERATELY NOT TAKEN: it buys the mitigation for the one scenario where a cheap retry is wrong (a stall that is really a network or system-sleep event, i.e. the 2 exhausted cases), because attempt 3 still waits 20 minutes before the final attempt. Short at the front, long at the back -- which is what the item asked for. **THE SHIP-BLOCKING TRAP THE SCOUT DID NOT FLAG, found by the PM lead before speccing.** Two existing assertions in `tests/test_iter129_behavior.py` PIN `stalled` to the long ladder and would have turned the suite red: `test_b8_every_other_kind_including_an_unknown_one_keeps_the_long_ladder` parametrizes `"stalled"` and asserts `[600, 1200, 2400]` (its docstring even says "`stalled` staying here is the spec's explicit out-of-scope decision"), and `test_b13_wait_helps_kinds_keep_todays_backoff_end_to_end` parametrizes `STALLED_BLOB` with the same expectation. The spec therefore mandates NARROWING both in place -- drop only `stalled`/`STALLED_BLOB` from the two parametrize lists, keep both function names verbatim, keep `"other"` + the unknown kind + `SERVICE_BLOB` + `UNMATCHED_BLOB` + `""`, keep `STALLED_BLOB` defined (behaviors 4/6/7/14 of that file still use it for CLASSIFICATION, which is unchanged), and correct the two header-docstring lines to record the reversal with a reason. That distinction is the whole point: a green suite bought by DELETING the assertion is the failure mode, and the spec makes the narrowing an explicit numbered behavior (13) so the reviewer can tell one from the other. **WHY THE SHAPE IS A MAP AND NOT A SET MEMBERSHIP.** Moving `stalled` into `FAST_RETRY_KINDS` was explicitly forbidden by the item, and it is also mechanically impossible without a red suite: `tests/test_iter129_behavior.py:288` pins that tuple's element set to exactly `{"timeout", "cli-error"}`. The map also gives the per-kind ladder a place to live that does not disturb the two frozen ladders (`BACKOFFS` is pinned by both `test_iter119` and `test_iter129`), and it makes the revert a SINGLE dict entry -- spec behavior 7(b) asserts that emptying the map restores iteration 129's numbers exactly. Totality was specified rather than left to the engineer: an explicitly-EMPTY mapped ladder must return `RETRY_DELAY_FLOOR` and must NOT fall through to `BACKOFFS` (an explicit-but-empty entry is not an absent one), the floor still clamps a mapped ladder, and the map is read from the module global by BARE NAME at CALL TIME so a `monkeypatch.setattr(foundry, "KIND_RETRY_LADDERS", ...)` bites -- the def-time-capture trap the learnings head names. **Deliberately OUT of scope, recorded so nothing re-derives it:** any new failure KIND (notably `auth`, now twice deferred) and any edit to `ATTEMPT_FAILURE_MARKERS` or `classify_attempt_failure` (`stalled` was ALREADY a classified kind since iteration 129 -- that is what made this a pricing change rather than a detection change); a SECOND map entry (`timeout`/`cli-error` already have a measured fast ladder and `service` must keep the long one, since a busy backend is the one failure where a long sleep is correct, so the map ships with exactly one key); any change to `MAX_ATTEMPTS`, `COOLDOWNS` or `sleep_interruptible` (de-listed by iteration 130's scout A: 12 of 17 timeout stage-runs recover on a retry, so cutting attempts trades visible waste for lost iterations); and any `foundry stage-times` change to surface the new ladder -- the deliverable is the changed DELAY, not a new report. **One inertness caveat, stated up front so it is never reported as "the fix did not work":** this is `foundry.py` code and the running dispatcher holds the imported module in memory (iteration 130's live-lag finding), so the new ladder takes effect on the first stage retry AFTER the operator restarts the dispatcher. That is also precisely why it is resume-safe -- no in-flight loop changes its retry semantics mid-shift, and `run_iteration`, the stage sequence, every sentinel string and every config field are untouched.

- **iter 136 -- the pinned steering head stops mailing four COMPLETED operator directives to every stage, and an over-budget head now reports itself through `doctor` (scout B's B1, narrative-and-docs lens).** Measured before deciding, not inferred: the `## Patterns` head of the gitignored `products/_platform/LEARNINGS.md` was 19 bullet blocks / 15,277 chars, inlined into EVERY stage prompt by `build_prompt` (foundry.py:12799-12806), and FOUR of those bullets were fully spent -- #3 (1,210 chars, the 2026-08-03 'ship bites 1-4 in order' directive; all four bites shipped and the strangler BLOCKED marker was already cleared), #5 (630, 'novelty-check is a brake with no pedal', wired since iter 105/113 at foundry.py:12829 + roles/pm.md:91, and its own evidence line `rg novelty roles/` was a CASE-SENSITIVITY false negative), #16 (1,437, 'give `learnings_digest` a character budget', shipped iter 118) and #17 (3,191, 'make the IPC endpoint self-healing via a connect probe', shipped iters 110 + 114) = 6,468 chars, 42.3% of the head, instructing every role in every stage to build four things that already existed. Under the shipped prompt bounds (800 per bullet / 10,000 total) the head emitted with 7 of 19 bullets TRUNCATED MID-INSTRUCTION and three of the seven were exactly the spent ones, so dead text was clipping LIVE rules below it; the PM verified by calling `_bound_head` directly that retiring the four takes the raw head to 8,805 chars / 4 truncated, and 9,155 / 4 once the required <=400-char POINTER bullet is added -- 16 bullets, 0 dropped. it also earns nothing until a human restart loads it), A2 concurrent scouts (two model calls on one account collides head-on with the VISION's single-brain constraint), A3 `-n auto` (real 46.6s->17.0s measurement, but it mutates the DECLARED quality-check command that the final gate and the fresh-clone post-release both consume -- `uv run --with pytest pytest -q` resolves only pytest into that env, so an addopts `-n auto` hard-fails there and the downside is a BROKEN post-release for a ~2min/iteration gain), B2 (the ARCHITECTURE.md:90 / CONTINUOUS.md:39 backoff figures, still true drift, left on the board), B3 (the `roles/pm.md` `lint-spec` invocation, already roadmap item (h) with its working form), a `foundry learnings-head` CLI verb (a read-only verb nobody consults is the failure mode the operator named twice -- the pedal is `doctor`), and ANY edit to `learnings_digest` / `_bound_head` / `build_prompt` / the `PROMPT_LEARNINGS_*` constants (the prompt PATH is read-only here; only the CONTENT of the steering file changes). **Two verification traps the spec pins because they are invisible in a diff:** `products/*/LEARNINGS.md` is GITIGNORED, so the head surgery contributes ZERO ship diff and had to be verified by MEASUREMENT reported in the stage logs (bullets == 16, raw_chars <= 9,300, truncated <= 4, dropped == 0, all four bullets still present verbatim, no archive line left-stripping to `- [`, unchanged total lesson count) rather than by review; and NO suite test may read that file or any gitignored path, because it is ABSENT in the fresh clone the post-release gate uses -- a test that reads it would ship a BROKEN post-release. The anti-drift oracle for the new helper is behavior 6: for the same text and caps its truncated/bullets/dropped must equal the numbers `learnings_digest` renders in its own `> [head bounded: ...]` notice, and an under-budget head must produce NO notice at all, so the audit can never silently diverge from the bound it reports on.

The hand-edit it then asked for is the other half of the cost: since iteration 128 the loader FAILS CLOSED on an unrecognised key, so a one-character typo (`test_command`, `push_enable`) aborts a shift. **What shipped:** a pure `product_config_template` (13 keys -- the ones `USAGE.md` tells an operator to edit, plus one `_comment` carrying its own instructions, exempt from the unknown-key guard via `CONFIG_COMMENT_PREFIX`), a pure `product_name_error` refusal gate, a pure `new_product_exit_code`, a pure `new_product_next_steps`, and `new_product_cli`, dispatched BEFORE `load_config` in `main()` on the `stage-times` precedent because the verb CREATES the config there is no config to load. **Three design choices with their WHY, so nothing re-derives them:** `work_root` is written EXPLICITLY as `{FOUNDRY}/products/<name>` because an empty value lets `ProductConfig.resolve()`'s default fire at LOAD time against whatever `FOUNDRY` is bound to, which is how a test with its own tmp root still mkdirs inside the real checkout; `push_enabled` is FALSE so a scaffold with an unreviewed `allowed_push_repo` and no VISION can never push; and `vision` is left EMPTY rather than guessed at `<repo>/VISION.md`, because a guessed path that does not exist yet is a lint ERROR (a scaffolder that ships broken) while an unset one is the WARN "vision path is unset" -- i.e. the scaffold's own lint output IS the operator's to-do list. **The one judgement call a reviewer should weigh:** the exit code is scoped to `NEW_PRODUCT_ARG_FIELDS` (`name`, `repo`, `allowed_push_repo`, `test_cmd`), each mapping 1:1 to a flag, so a non-zero exit is always fixable by re-running with different arguments; the full `ConfigLint.render()` is still printed and nothing is hidden, but checkout-level findings (`roles_dir`) and not-yet-made edits (`vision`) cannot set it -- `lint-config` remains the whole-config verdict and the printed next steps name it. **Deliberately OUT of scope:** `--json` (the pre-declared successor bite), any edit to the dispatcher roster (the verb PRINTS a paste-ready `work_items` snippet, because a roster change only takes effect on restart and a CLI that rewrote it would manufacture the "I changed the roster and nothing happened" surprise), `foundry pin`/`retire`, and any change to `load_config`/`resolve`/`lint_config`/`ProductConfig`. **Recorded because the ENGINEER wrote this bullet, not the PM:** the PM stage was killed at the ~600s cap after it had checkpointed `pm.md` and the one-line `PLATFORM_ROADMAP.md` ledger row but BEFORE it wrote this archive bullet, which left the iter-122/124 archive-gap brake RED (`roadmap_archive_gaps` returned `[137]`) and the suite failing on two tests at the start of the engineer stage; the engineer wrote the bullet to satisfy the spec's own acceptance criterion rather than shipping a green feature on a red brake.

Those 4 are OPERATOR directives, the one channel by which a human steers every role in the fleet, so the loss is 2,356 chars of the highest-precedence text in the prompt (delivered digest 13,298 chars now vs 15,654 with the bullet cap lifted, and the entire delta is head loss -- the lesson tail measured fully compliant at 7,943 of 10,000, so no lesson is truncated today). The bug is the ORDER, documented in `_bound_head` as deliberate: truncation is applied FIRST and the budget SECOND, mirroring `lesson_chars`-then-`max_chars`. That order is correct for the lesson tail (many short lines, admitted newest-first) and WRONG for the head, because it makes the cheapest possible head -- one that already fits -- pay the most destructive bound. Fix: when the raw head fits the total budget, emit it verbatim; only when it overflows does the existing truncate-then-drop path run, byte-for-byte as today.
